| `Ortho4XP/tools/tunnel_portal_acceptance.py` | You have an emitted patch from a TUNNEL round and need the acceptance battery those rounds are judged on, as ONE instrument: a `tunnel_ramp` surface within reach of each named portal site and a ramp VERTEX at the mouth, no below-grade vertex over a mapped bore's COVERED span, no `tunnel_low_connector`, no altitude-needle way inside a control footprint, a building pad still FLAT, sub-grade node counts per role at or under the control's, ways BYTE-IDENTICAL in geometry to a control twin whose altitudes moved, `authority_retreat_wall` improvising at a ramp edge, over-cap `within_shape` rows on `tunnel_ramp`, rows minted by a ROLE-LESS interior ring, and the matched ADJUDICATED delta + ACTIONABLE SITES. Promoted 2026-08-07 from the lane scratchpad `accept_othh.py` on its SECOND use (RULINGS `7e90032`, promote-on-reuse) — two rounds asked the same questions and the second re-derived the geometry-drift class under a DIFFERENT identity predicate, reading 148 and 164 for one population (the two-instruments trap, memory `two-instruments-one-assumed-population`). **It counts nothing itself**: every row count is `harness/census.py`'s `census_one` (= `check_grade.run_checks_law_true`), the adjudicated total is the census's own `adjudicated_total` field, and the patch is read through `check_grade._parse_osm` — a private re-count is the census-wrapper defect. Thresholds are ARGUMENTS (`--over-cap-ramp-max`, `--adjudicated-delta-max`, `--actionable-sites-max`, `--drift-max`, `--retreat-wall-max`, `--site-max-m`, `--mouth-max-m`, `--needle-spread-m`), so a round restates its own bar instead of editing a check; sites come from a shipped `--profile` (OTHH) or repeatable `--site NAME=LAT,LON`, never a literal inside a check. The identity join is the canonical 11-decimal coordinate SET with ties broken by way id — `most_common` alone broke them by set-iteration order and read 8 and 9 on the same bytes. A check whose inputs are absent reports **SKIPPED**, never PASS. It NO LONGER answers "does R14-1's claim name this bore?": `claim_names_the_bore` and `--claim-cover-min` are RETIRED with the `tunnel_road` claim class itself (RULINGS 2026-08-31b, linear-transport redesign spec §5.1). That check is what refuted the claim-scoped designs — the bore ring measured 0-2 of 33 nodes inside the claim and 14 of its welds were partners at ZERO coverage against 3 claimed, so no rule keyed on claim membership could reach the carriers — and the retirement is its verdict carried out. Every bore question is now keyed on RAMP/MOUTH GEOMETRY: `site_reach` admits `tunnel_ramp` role ways and `tunnel_mouth` ref ways, and `claimed_corridor_walls` is `bore_corridor_walls` over the below-grade `tunnel_ramp` population (bar `--claim-wall-cover-min`, unchanged). Re-keyed in the SAME batch as the retirement, so the OTHH battery cannot silently go SKIP against a patch with no claims left. It also enumerates **THE CANONICAL TUNNEL MOUTH** (`mouth_inventory`, added 2026-08-31 for redesign Batch 3, EXTENDING this instrument rather than forking a second one). A MOUTH SITE is a cluster of the tunnel's OWN emitted road surfaces (role `tunnel_ramp` plus the corridor refs, EXCLUDING `object_bridge_ramp` — a classified hard-deck OBJECT bridge is governed by the object law, R14-2/A-3's first exception, and counting it reported a "mouth" with no ramp and no walls) standing within `--mouth-cluster-m` of each other — NOT a `tunnel_mouth` way: the first cut keyed on that ref and measured ZERO on the very airport it adjudicates, because the OTHH control (2,600 shapes) carries 22 ramp surfaces, 39 `tunnel_wall` band pieces and not one `tunnel_mouth` or `tunnel_cap` way — the 2026-08-30j merge note says so in words ("wrapped ends = end cap"), and a check whose population is empty where it adjudicates is the silent-SKIP failure. Per site it reports the corridor SURFACES standing there (ramp / mouth plate / other / fragment below `--corridor-fragment-m2`), whether the ramp REACHES the plate (`--mouth-reach-m`), the `tunnel_wall` BAND pieces per SIDE of the site's own principal axis (the covariance's dominant eigenvector over its coordinates — the corridor's direction is the direction its own surface runs, so no second notion of a corridor and no build state is needed), the END CAP as either a `tunnel_cap` way or the WRAPPED END the 2026-08-30j merge accepted for it (the share of the site's perimeter no band piece answers, bar `--mouth-open-frac-max`), the DUPLICATE corridor surfaces (two tunnel road surfaces sharing >= `--dup-overlap-m2` — the class R14-1's retired claim minted beside the synthetic ramp), and the NESTED and FRAGMENT band pieces (`--wall-fragment-m2`), all within `--mouth-radius-m`. RULINGS 2026-08-30's law stated as a measurement: 1 ramp surface, 1 wall band per side, a cap or a wrapped end, 0 duplicates, 0 nested/fragment pieces (the per-side FOOT column left with the §T5 foot itself — RULINGS 2026-09-01c). Each site line carries its own lat/lon (inverted through the patch's OWN anchor and `R_EARTH`, so a reviewer flies to where the instrument measured). The full inventory PRINTS whatever the verdict — "quote every mouth" is the acceptance, not a single number — and with no `--mouth-canonical` the verdict is SKIPPED, never PASS. Twin: `tests/test_tunnel_mouth_inventory.py`. Twin: `tests/test_tunnel_portal_acceptance.py` (the CLI's JSON IS the library entry's result; no private re-count or second parser; SKIPPED-not-PASS; thresholds move the verdict and not the measurement; the index entry itself). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/lattice_overlap_read.py` | The question is CONTAINMENT — *does an emitted apron-membrane segment leave the apron it belongs to?* — and no other instrument asks it. `harness/census.py` prices PAIRS OF VALUES, so a breakline running straight through a carved building breaks no grade law and reports ZERO rows however wrong it looks in the sim; `osm_site.py` says what is at one coordinate and `arm_site_read.py` answers one named place, but neither sweeps a whole patch asking containment. This is that sweep, over the two role-less membrane feature classes (`apron_lattice`, `apron_spine_station`), naming for each offending segment the metres outside and the (role, way id, metres) it passes through. **It prices no law and counts no defects** — the apron footprint, the metre frame about the sidecar's own anchor and the role-carrying rings all come from the harness library (`check_grade._parse_osm` / `_ll_to_m_factory`), imported and never re-spelled (the census-wrapper precedent); defect counts come from `harness/census.py` and nowhere else. ONE DELIBERATE EXCEPTION, and it is the reason the tool exists in this shape: **`check_grade._parse_osm` DROPS ANY WAY WITH FEWER THAN THREE NODES** before its open-feature route, and a two-node membrane breakline is exactly what a short apron crossing emits — read through that parser alone, 13 of 18 HECA station crossings were invisible and the read called an apron stationless while the patch carried stations on it. So the FEATURE ways are parsed directly here; the footprint they are judged against is still the library's. `--tolerance` is emit rounding (default 0.05 m), NOT a law threshold. Measured basis (RULINGS 2026-08-26b item 1, the owner's sim read of 1.0.260): 7 of 970 HECA `apron_lattice` segments left the apron footprint, 89.6 m — 28.1 m through building way -10158, 23.5 m through junctions -12775/-12776, the rest through graded strips; after the round-3 §2 per-segment clip the same read returns 0. Promoted 2026-08-27 from the round3spine lane's scratchpad on its ~8th use (RULINGS `7e90032`, promote-on-reuse). Several patches are reported separately — the arm-to-arm read; quote it on identical options, never as a verdict. Twin: `tests/test_lattice_overlap_read.py` (inside-is-not-reported, leaving-is, what-it-passes-through, the 2-node way the census parser drops, the tolerance both ways, prices-no-law, frame-from-the-library, the no-sidecar refusal, the CLI's JSON IS the library result, and this index row). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/tunnel_portal_acceptance.py` | You have an emitted patch from a TUNNEL round and need the acceptance battery those rounds are judged on, as ONE instrument: a `tunnel_ramp` surface within reach of each named portal site and a ramp VERTEX at the mouth, no below-grade vertex over a mapped bore's COVERED span, no `tunnel_low_connector`, no altitude-needle way inside a control footprint, a building pad still FLAT, sub-grade node counts per role at or under the control's, ways BYTE-IDENTICAL in geometry to a control twin whose altitudes moved, `authority_retreat_wall` improvising at a ramp edge, over-cap `within_shape` rows on `tunnel_ramp`, rows minted by a ROLE-LESS interior ring, and the matched ADJUDICATED delta + ACTIONABLE SITES. Promoted 2026-08-07 from the lane scratchpad `accept_othh.py` on its SECOND use (RULINGS `7e90032`, promote-on-reuse) — two rounds asked the same questions and the second re-derived the geometry-drift class under a DIFFERENT identity predicate, reading 148 and 164 for one population (the two-instruments trap, memory `two-instruments-one-assumed-population`). **It counts nothing itself**: every row count is `harness/census.py`'s `census_one` (= `check_grade.run_checks_law_true`), the adjudicated total is the census's own `adjudicated_total` field, and the patch is read through `check_grade._parse_osm` — a private re-count is the census-wrapper defect. Thresholds are ARGUMENTS (`--over-cap-ramp-max`, `--adjudicated-delta-max`, `--actionable-sites-max`, `--drift-max`, `--retreat-wall-max`, `--site-max-m`, `--mouth-max-m`, `--needle-spread-m`), so a round restates its own bar instead of editing a check; sites come from a shipped `--profile` (OTHH, LEMD) or repeatable `--site NAME=LAT,LON`, never a literal inside a check. `--site` mode also takes the BORE inputs (`--bore-osm`, `--bore-ways`, `--covered-span`, added 2026-08-28 by the tunnel-integrity round §T8.2) so an ad-hoc run executes the covered-span and claim checks instead of skipping them; a bore source may be an OSM extract OR an `_airport_road_feed/*.cache` road-feed pickle, which is where an airport with no `big_roads` tunnel extract actually keeps its bores. `covered_span_clean` measures against a **LOCAL grade datum** — the median altitude of the emitted vertices in the annulus beside the corridor (`--datum-ring-m`, `--datum-min-samples`, `--below-grade-m`), not absolute 0.0: the old absolute predicate was structurally vacuous on any field above sea level and reported PASS over a span it had not examined, and an undeclared span or a corridor with no annulus evidence now reports SKIPPED. The identity join is the canonical 11-decimal coordinate SET with ties broken by way id — `most_common` alone broke them by set-iteration order and read 8 and 9 on the same bytes. A check whose inputs are absent reports **SKIPPED**, never PASS. It NO LONGER answers "does R14-1's claim name this bore?": `claim_names_the_bore` and `--claim-cover-min` are RETIRED with the `tunnel_road` claim class itself (RULINGS 2026-08-31b, linear-transport redesign spec §5.1). That check is what refuted the claim-scoped designs — the bore ring measured 0-2 of 33 nodes inside the claim and 14 of its welds were partners at ZERO coverage against 3 claimed, so no rule keyed on claim membership could reach the carriers — and the retirement is its verdict carried out. Every bore question is now keyed on RAMP/MOUTH GEOMETRY: `site_reach` admits `tunnel_ramp` role ways and `tunnel_mouth` ref ways, and `claimed_corridor_walls` is `bore_corridor_walls` over the below-grade `tunnel_ramp` population (bar `--claim-wall-cover-min`, unchanged). Re-keyed in the SAME batch as the retirement, so the OTHH battery cannot silently go SKIP against a patch with no claims left. It also enumerates **THE CANONICAL TUNNEL MOUTH** (`mouth_inventory`, added 2026-08-31 for redesign Batch 3, EXTENDING this instrument rather than forking a second one). A MOUTH SITE is a cluster of the tunnel's OWN emitted road surfaces (role `tunnel_ramp` plus the corridor refs, EXCLUDING `object_bridge_ramp` — a classified hard-deck OBJECT bridge is governed by the object law, R14-2/A-3's first exception, and counting it reported a "mouth" with no ramp and no walls) standing within `--mouth-cluster-m` of each other — NOT a `tunnel_mouth` way: the first cut keyed on that ref and measured ZERO on the very airport it adjudicates, because the OTHH control (2,600 shapes) carries 22 ramp surfaces, 39 `tunnel_wall` band pieces and not one `tunnel_mouth` or `tunnel_cap` way — the 2026-08-30j merge note says so in words ("wrapped ends = end cap"), and a check whose population is empty where it adjudicates is the silent-SKIP failure. Per site it reports the corridor SURFACES standing there (ramp / mouth plate / other / fragment below `--corridor-fragment-m2`), whether the ramp REACHES the plate (`--mouth-reach-m`), the `tunnel_wall` BAND pieces per SIDE of the site's own principal axis (the covariance's dominant eigenvector over its coordinates — the corridor's direction is the direction its own surface runs, so no second notion of a corridor and no build state is needed), the END CAP as either a `tunnel_cap` way or the WRAPPED END the 2026-08-30j merge accepted for it (the share of the site's perimeter no band piece answers, bar `--mouth-open-frac-max`), the DUPLICATE corridor surfaces (two tunnel road surfaces sharing >= `--dup-overlap-m2` — the class R14-1's retired claim minted beside the synthetic ramp), and the NESTED and FRAGMENT band pieces (`--wall-fragment-m2`), all within `--mouth-radius-m`. RULINGS 2026-08-30's law stated as a measurement: 1 ramp surface, 1 wall band per side, a cap or a wrapped end, 0 duplicates, 0 nested/fragment pieces (the per-side FOOT column left with the §T5 foot itself — RULINGS 2026-09-01c). Each site line carries its own lat/lon (inverted through the patch's OWN anchor and `R_EARTH`, so a reviewer flies to where the instrument measured). The full inventory PRINTS whatever the verdict — "quote every mouth" is the acceptance, not a single number — and with no `--mouth-canonical` the verdict is SKIPPED, never PASS. Twin: `tests/test_tunnel_mouth_inventory.py`. Twin: `tests/test_tunnel_portal_acceptance.py` (the CLI's JSON IS the library entry's result; no private re-count or second parser; SKIPPED-not-PASS; thresholds move the verdict and not the measurement; the index entry itself). |
