The LEMD ramp/road fidelity round (2026-08-28) added **`wall_top_flat`** (§F1 — THE WALL TOP IS FLAT ACROSS ITS WIDTH: the worst `|Δalt|` between two `tunnel_wall` band vertices standing closer than `--wall-band-span-m` (default 2.0 m) in plan, which on a ~1 m band whose stations stand tens of metres apart is ACROSS the band by construction and never along its run — the read the owner made by hand on the 1.0.265 patch, 610.6/610.1, 608.3/607.7, 606.2/605.4. One vertex per NODE, joined on the canonical 11-decimal spelling, so a ring's closing repeat cannot put a guaranteed 0.00 into the population. Measured on the LEMD control: 0.90 m worst over 482 cross-band pairs on 21 band ways. With no `--wall-top-delta-max` it REPORTS (SKIPPED) rather than adjudicating, which is how the attribution arms read it and how every pre-§F1 profile keeps reading). Twin: `tests/test_tunnel_portal_acceptance.py` (the CLI's JSON IS the library entry's result; no private re-count or second parser; SKIPPED-not-PASS; thresholds move the verdict and not the measurement; the index entry itself). |
| `Ortho4XP/tests/test_harness.py` | The harness's own twins. Run these after touching any harness entry or `check_grade`'s law register. |

**The shared data repo (owner ruling `e9daef5`).**
`/Users/noah/XPTerrainBuilderData` is THE data repo: DEM + insets, OSM
extracts + road feeds, airport mod cache, geotiffs, masks, DSF cache,
orthophotos. Every lane MOUNTS it via the ritual — never copies, never
keeps a private cache. Downloads and cache regenerations happen EXACTLY
ONCE as explicit, locked, hash-stamped events
(`build_airport.py --refresh-data <scope>`), never as a build side effect;
the refresh ledger lives in the repo at `.harness/refresh_ledger.jsonl`.
A build that writes there without authorisation is reported and its run is
marked CONTAMINATED.

`check_grade.py` is the harness's library: `LAW_FAMILIES`,
`law_context_from_sidecar`, `run_checks(family_out=...)`,
`run_checks_law_true`, `row_side`. Import those rather than re-deriving
them anywhere.

## Acceptance gates — pass/fail against a law or spec

| Tool | Reach for it when |
|---|---|
| `scripts/check_frozen_tile.py` (+ `.sh`) | You are proving a FROZEN release bundle can build a tile and SOLVE an airport through the shipped JSONL protocol (`--pass tile|airport|both`), or you are chasing a CROSS-PLATFORM divergence in that solve. `--xplat-dump DIR` arms the engine's own per-stage digest writer (`auto_patch_v2/pipeline/xplat.py`, `O4_V2_XPLAT_DIGEST`) and copies `CYXY.xplat.json` + the report + the patch out of the throwaway fixture before it is deleted; the release job uploads that directory on SUCCESS from all three platforms. `--compare NAME=PATH …` then prints the per-stage AGREE/DIFFER table (load, partition, classify, planar, shapes, constraints, lp, solved) with the numbers and names the FIRST divergent stage, exit 2. Every digest is a sorted-line sha256 taken at 9/6/4/2/1 dp, so a last-ulp difference (fine rounding only) is distinguishable from a topology change (every rounding), and no enumeration order can move it. The driver needs NOTHING but the standard library — it never derives a number, the bundle under test does. Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/auto_patch_v2/test_xplat_digest.py`. Measured basis (lane `xplatdeterminism`, 2026-09-17): two runs of one macOS bundle agree at 9 dp at EVERY stage, and the CI macOS bundle (py3.13, frozen, GitHub runner) agrees with a local macOS SOURCE run (py3.14, venv, another machine) at every stage through `lp` — so the divergence is the ARCHITECTURE, not ordering, packaging or the Python version. Run 35272775466 named the first divergent stage `load`: identical counts, geometry equal at 4 dp and apart at 6 dp, Linux == Windows there and macOS apart, on identical PROJ 9.5.1 / GEOS 3.13.1 / numpy 2.4.4 — PROJ's compiled forward tmerc, last ulp; `classify` then came out 105 / 104 / 105 cells and the three solved 17128x1933 / 17151x1922 / 17039x1934. Run 35274144555 is the INTERVENTIONAL arm (projection quantised to 0.1 mm, commit `8615f4f9`): load, classify, planar (DEM included), shapes and every constraint count identical on all three, LP exactly 17096 x 1926 / 275 rounds everywhere. **The EXACT spread (lane `xplatspread`):** `--xplat-dump` also writes `CYXY.xproj.json` — every load-stage `to_xy` as `float.hex()` IN and OUT (join key = the input pair, identical everywhere by construction, so the join is exact and never proximity), a deterministic lattice probe forward and inverse out to ~14 km (hub scale), and the solved z keyed by its vertex's xy. `--compare-projection NAME=PATH …` prints, per platform pair and per axis, max / p99 / median `|Δ|` in metres, the decade histogram, the growth with distance from the frame origin, and the STRADDLE counts at 1e-4 / 1e-3 / 1e-2 m and at **0.5 m** — the two coarser ones being the grids the pipeline ALREADY snaps to (`classify` `[cells] snap_grid_m` = 0.01 m, `classify/rules.toml:8`; the planar arrangement's `grid_size` = `emit.identity.min_distinct_spacing_m` = 0.5 m, `planar/overlay.py:360-362, 441, 476`), per axis, per coordinate and for z, with the straddling coordinates NAMED in lat/lon so they can be cross-referenced with the stage dumps' first divergence, and the expected count projected to 10x / 50x CYXY's N — the numbers owner Q 17d-1's grid choice rests on. `--xplat-quantise M` (default 1e-3, 0 disables) runs a SECOND, NON-GATING solve with the load stage's projection snapped, i.e. every platform fed identical inputs, into `<dump>/quantised`. Byte-neutral when off and when merely armed: CYXY 17128 x 1933, 268 rounds, 835,585 B on all three of OFF / armed / pre-lane. Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/auto_patch_v2/test_xplat_spread.py`. MEASURED (runs 35283889554 and 35285038635, CYXY, N = 2,847 load coordinates): the real spread of `to_xy` is **~4e-10 m mean, 2.1e-9 m max** — NANOMETRES, three to five decades finer than 17d's "(5e-7, 5e-5) m" bracket, which over-read a dp6 digest (a digest differs when ANY of 2,847 values straddles the 1e-6 rounding, expected ~2). It is almost all NORTHING (x mean 1.5e-14, y 3.8e-10) and does NOT grow with distance from the origin out to 14 km. STRADDLES at 1e-4 / 1e-3 / 1e-2 / 0.5 m: **0 of 2,847 on every platform pair** — so the existing 1 cm classify and 0.5 m planar grids are NOT where the divergence is minted. Linux and Windows are NOT equal (84 of 2,847 coordinates apart by ~1.4e-9 m); the inverse `to_ll` differs on 18 of 289 probe points by ≤1.9e-9 m. With the load projection snapped to 1 mm, every stage through `shapes` agrees on all three, LP is 17289 x 1918 / 181 rounds everywhere, matched constraint rows agree to ≤8e-13 m, and the emitted patch BODY is BYTE-IDENTICAL on all three (header excluded, CRLF normalised). **§46 THE GATE (lane `xplatquantum`, 2026-09-17).** The quantum now SHIPS — `emit.identity.input_quantum_m` = 1 mm, applied at `model/frame.Frame.entry()` — so `--xplat-quantise M` no longer runs a second quantiser: it OVERRIDES the law value for a measurement arm, `0` being the pre-§46 UNQUANTISED control, and PASS 2b runs only when it is given (omitted = no second solve, which is ~4 min of CI per platform back). `--compare` now also compares the EMITTED ARTEFACTS byte for byte beside the stage table — `CYXY_auto.patch.osm` (the `<osm>` header line excluded: it carries the runner's temp path) and `CYXY.graded.json`, both CRLF-normalised, with a CR named as a 17g regression — and it runs that comparison EVEN WHEN a stage differs, because "did it reach the shipped file?" is the question a gate stopping at the first digest cannot answer. `--gate` applies §46 (7)'s release bar instead of the human one: any divergence at load / partition / classify / planar / shapes, any stage COUNT, or a non-identical emitted artefact is exit 2; the §46 (6) residues (`lp counts.nnz`, `constraints rows.*`, `solved z.*`) are printed BY NAME and allowed, a list to shrink, never to grow. That is the fourth `release.yml` job (`xplat_gate`, needs the three platform jobs, downloads the three `frozen-tile-logs-*`), and the `release` job needs it. MEASURED on run 35285038635's own artefacts, which is what the shipped quantum reproduces: in the 1 mm arm the emitted patch body is BYTE-IDENTICAL on all three (`f59f88c78422`, 830,345 B) and the gate passes; unquantised the same comparison gives three different bodies (`b30569c469fe` / `ec67813e3fb1` / `e73f1f934fe4`) and ten failing LP counts. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/check_grade.py` | You want the grade validator's CLI on one patch, or its library from code. The CLI is a thin front end over the same law reader the census uses. A run with no sidecar is CONTEXT-FREE and overcounts — it is not a defect count. It also prints **THE COCKPIT BLOCK FIRST** (owner RULINGS 2026-09-12x/12y; §31 (6)) — the same `cockpit_block` / `cockpit_block_lines` the harness census and the pytest fixtures call, over the SAME run's `family_out` (the checks' own output is buffered and replayed under the block, never run twice). A `strip_seam_tear` row carries the PAIR MIDPOINT as its lat/lon (spec §32 (3), RULINGS 2026-09-12ag): it used to carry none, and `run_checks` filled it with the offending way's RING CENTROID — at LEMD that sent the cockpit block's first CRITICAL VISUAL find 220 m from the 8.25 m tear. Twinned both sides (`tests/auto_patch_v2/test_v2zoneclamp.py`), the engine's own `verify/strips.strip_seam_tear` alongside. The block's "in view by approach" test is the ONE approach corridor of `auto_patch_v2.law.approach_corridor` (RULINGS 2026-09-12al; twins `tests/auto_patch_v2/test_v2approachcorridor.py`), never a radius around a runway vertex. **`pad_airside_renode`** (spec §16g (10) (12) (2), Fable 2026-09-16 / RULINGS 2026-09-16b, lane `v2padclip`) is the PRESENCE family the airside vertex set had none of: one row per airside CELL vertex the PAD STAGE minted or deleted, read from the sidecar's `pad_airside_renode` (`[lat, lon, "minted"|"deleted"]`, published by `planar/overlay.build_arrangement` comparing its own pass A — the airside noded before any pad exists — with its pass B). Sidecar-declared like `eat_ceiling` / `seam_pins`, because the only place both node sets exist is inside the build's own arrangement; an ABSENT key is NOT MEASURED and an EMPTY list is MEASURED ZERO. No other family sees it: an emitted surface carrying an extra airside vertex breaks no grade law, which is why the clip alone deleting 1,082 solve-owned airside vertices and minting 235 at HECA (RULINGS 2026-09-15ah) had to be attributed with a lane arm. Class `keepout` — one node is the whole defect and there is no magnitude to threshold. Twins: `tests/auto_patch_v2/test_v2padvert.py` (both directions, the densifier filter, the publication's absent-vs-empty contract). **`adjacent_ground_step`** (spec §34 (4), lane `v2rampwalk` 2026-09-13) is the WITHIN-FACE welded step on a v2 `adjacent_ground:*` face — the reading no family had: `graded_strip` carries no within-shape cap, `adjacent_ground_tear` fires only under a 1 m edge and `strip_seam_tear` is the CROSS-shape twin, so a band holding its designed level over a mapped road's own ground (LEMD `zone2#2`, 1.73 m over 1.5 m at road −6289) was priced by nothing. Its floor is the cockpit's own `visual_m` AND `cliff_grade` in one step: without the cliff term it counts the lawful hillside drape (measured CYXY 296 rows). Lockstep both sides — `auto_patch_v2.verify.strips.adjacent_ground_step` reads it in the engine; twins `tests/auto_patch_v2/test_v2rampwalk.py` and the v1/v2 census parity test. **`ramp_in_road`** (spec §34 (10), owner RULINGS 2026-09-14bb/14bc/14bd, lane `v2othhramp`) is the CRITICAL presence family the road margin needed: every ramp arriving at a road ends at the road's TRUE edge — the centreline offset by the road's own half-width toward the ramp, ONE derivation `planar/wall_corridor_ramps.road_true_edge` read by every ramp emitter — so a RAMP vertex standing INSIDE a road ribbon is a lane of carriageway cut away, whatever its elevation. No other family sees it: a ramp welded flat into the road it ate breaks no grade law and prices zero rows. A vertex within the census's own weld tolerance of the ribbon's edge is ON the edge, which is what the law asks for. It reads 0 at OTHH before and after — the family is the GUARD on that derivation and never a defect count. Twins: `tests/test_harness.py` §34 (10) (both directions, the weld line both ways, the register and the cockpit class, and the ramp-role set read from the law's own structure roles). **`ramp_in_strip`** (spec §34 (5) (b), Fable 2026-09-15 / RULINGS 2026-09-15h, owner 15e item 7, lane `v2lemdstruct2`) is its AIRSIDE sibling: the covered extent of an underpass beneath a taxiway or runway spans the pavement AND its graded strip, so a RAMP vertex standing inside that strip is a trench in the ground an aircraft leaving the pavement runs out onto — LEMD 40.4611623,-3.5444804, where a code-E strip is 19.0 m and the trench face stood at 15.5 m under a 5.42 m unbanked drop, the airport's worst CRITICAL VISUAL row. The strip region is ONE derivation with `planar/zones.zone_regions` and `planar/structure_underpass.strip_half_width_m` (the zone-2 half width for the cell's class, the zone-1 LIP where the class declares none), read from the LAW with a literal no-engine fallback the twin asserts against. TWO READINGS MEASURED, NOT CHOSEN: the face's sidecar HOLES are applied and the pavement SOLID is subtracted, so the region is the BAND — read ring-blind and disc-shaped the first arm reported 52 LEMD rows, every one inside `cross_connector:pav61`'s own 144,429 m2 void and up to 220 m from any kerb, and `de_m` can now never exceed the class's own half width. Twins: `tests/test_harness.py` §34 (5) (b) (both directions, the class's own half width against the zone law and the planar derivation, the taxiway-loop void, the weld line both ways, the register and the cockpit class). **THE RUNWAY SHOULDER'S OWN CAP** (spec §40 (2) as amended, owner RULINGS 2026-09-13dd, lane `v2roles`): a within-shape pair BOTH of whose nodes lie beyond their runway's own half width is priced at `shoulder_transverse_max` (2.5 %, ICAO Annex 14 §3.2.4), not the runway's 1.5 % — a §40 (1) SHOULDER keeps the runway's DATUM, not its cross-fall. The line is the SOLVE's and is read, never re-derived: the sidecar's `runway_axes` (`[ref, lat_a, lon_a, lat_b, lon_b, half_m]` off the apt.dat ends and width) and `shoulder_transverse_max`, through `shoulder_nids` / `_shoulder_cap`. Fitting the width to the runway RINGS instead would read a shoulder as part of the runway and never find its own line. A patch with no key reads exactly as before. One reading with the generator and the v2 verify (`auto_patch_v2.law.tables.runway_transverse_cap`); twins `tests/auto_patch_v2/test_runway_shoulder.py`. | **ITS LAW MACHINERY IS ITS OWN SINCE 2026-09-17** (lane `v1retire` round 1, seam S4 of RULINGS 2026-09-13aw, session ruling (d)): it read the pair law, the constraint generator, the seam law, the role vocabulary and the lateral machinery out of ELEVEN modules of the retired v1 engine, so the census could not survive the deletion. What it USES is copied ONCE into `tools/harness/law_support/` (`roles`, `strip_seam`, `grade_law`, `grade_graph`, `contiguity`, `corridor`, `transect` — 218 definitions / ~5.3k lines, verbatim), every law VALUE still IMPORTED from `auto_patch/config.py` (KEEP) or `auto_patch_v2/law/*.toml`, never re-spelled. The one deliberate change is the four `fabric_flags.on(...)` reads inside the adjacent-ground envelope, collapsed to their DEFAULT-ON arm (every flag in that registry is default-on and its OFF arm is pre-W2 v1 behaviour; v2 reads none of them). ACCEPTANCE: a census A/B over the SAME patch bytes (`/tmp/harness/xq_base_heca.osm`, 63,820 law-true rows) BYTE-IDENTICAL JSON before and after. Twin: `tests/test_law_support.py` (every copied definition IS the v1 definition while v1 is on disk, the flag collapse is exactly four default-on reads, the role tags are v2's own `precedence.toml` names, and the package imports no v1 module).
| `Ortho4XP/tools/check_build_time.py` | A change may have cost build time. Makes the build-time budgets executable (≤ 60 s per airport, ≤ 300 s per tile, cold, download-excluded). **Never wrap it in the ledger** — its output is a time. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/chain_divergence_audit.py` | Suspecting T-vertices / divergent shared chains (the class that took CYXY from 26 k to 1.55 M triangles), or measuring any of the round-16 GEOMETRY-CONSISTENCY classes on an emitted patch: TWIN-RING PAIRS (one boundary spelled twice by two rings whose differing vertices sit within `--chord-tol` of the partner chord — the zero-width constrained lens the +25+051 crash was minted by), SUB-MICRON CLUSTERS (distinct node ids within `--cluster-tol-deg`, default 1e-9 deg — the r15 cluster counter, promoted here on its second use rather than forked), NEEDLE TIPS (ring corners at or below `--needle-deg`, reported with the HEIGHT that decides whether the emitter's 0.09 m near-collinear repair can even see them) and UNOWNED WALL STRIPS (wall nodes >= 1 m above a ramp within 2 m with a gap between the two boundaries — ground no shape owns, which the mesh drapes at DEM/Z0). One implementation, `geometry_consistency()`, is shared by the CLI, the acceptance runs and the twins — a second, slightly-different counter is the census-wrapper defect. Twins: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_chain_divergence_audit.py`, `Ortho4XP/tests/test_chain_divergence_selfcross.py`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/crossing_zone_conformance.py` | Checking the crossing-terrain-ownership spec's phase-1 invariant. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/check_connector_coverage.py` | Checking that every apt.dat truck route is covered by emitted pavement. **Unguarded: importing it runs a build.** |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/airport_inset_acceptance.py` | Accepting an airport-elevation-inset / working-grid change through a real tile's steps 1–2. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/compare_target.py` | Scoring a produced layout against a hand-crafted target OSM. **`--iou-shape-id ID [--iou-envelope PATCH]` (2026-08-28) is the SCOPED IoU**: the TARGET's way with that `shapeID` is the REFERENCE EXTENT (joined by the tag, never the way id — JOSM renumbers ids), `--iou-envelope` supplies the FOOTPRINT the question is asked inside (the patch the reference was cut from, because after a severance the emitted pieces carry different shapeIDs and no tag join survives), and the RETAINED region is the OUTPUT's same-role union clipped to that footprint. Reports `IoU(retained, reference)` plus both cut areas and both one-sided residuals. Added for the HECA round-4 §H3 acceptance (the owner's reference surgery of apron 582); promoted from the hecar2 lane's `surgery_diff` scratch reader on its SECOND use per RULINGS `7e90032` — a parameter on the near-fit, never a fork. Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_compare_target.py`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/apron_pull_attrib.py` | A pass moved thousands of nodes and the emitted surface did not move — *what put them back?*  A census cannot answer it (a node returning to the DEM breaks no law), `harness/who_wrote.py` needs a BUILD to record the writes and its emitted-frame half only reads a CONSTANT-DEM world, and `arm_site_read.py` answers one coordinate.  This reads an emitted patch plus its sidecar and, for each apron-interior node the scaffold lifted but the build shipped near the terrain, names the BINDING CONSTRAINT AT EXIT in a declared precedence: `band` (the sidecar's own `band_clamp_nodes` / `band_excess`), `transect` (a bound `xsection_spans` row whose far side is on the DEM), `weld_ground` (a TIGHT enforced edge — one carrying its whole budget — to a non-apron neighbour sitting on the terrain: the membrane hung from a DEM edge), `weld_apron` (the same pull propagated inside the apron) and `unbound` (NO enforced edge is tight — nothing held it, the value simply returned there, which is the projection-convergence class).  **It derives no law and counts no defects**: geometry and the metre frame come from `check_grade._parse_osm` / `_ll_to_m_factory` about the sidecar anchor, the DEM through `apron_drape_read`'s loaders (hence `flat_site_sweep`'s, hence the engine's own `dem.alt`), the enforced graph VERBATIM from the sidecar's `pair_caps`, and the seed replay from the engine's own `law_graph_budget.build_anchor_envelope` + `scaffold_seed.taut_level`, so the replayed value is the seed's own arithmetic. **Two limits to quote with any number it prints.** (1) Seed values are a REPLAY, not a record — per-node seeds are not emitted, so anchors are read from the EMITTED spine and pad values rather than the phase-A ones the build used; the classification depends on the sign and rough size of the lift, never its third decimal. (2) EMITTED FRAME ONLY — the decimators mean most solver nodes never reach a patch (HECA: ~1.6 k emitted apron nodes carry a replayed seed against ~22 k re-seated in the solve), so a small selected population is a statement about the emitted subset and NOT evidence that the class is small. Added 2026-08-24 for the scaffold-seed round's decisive read, where it found the premise refuted: emitted-minus-seed was +0.01 m median (nothing pulls the surface back — the seed is honoured) and the apron sits on the terrain because its ANCHORS do. **`--way ID [--way ...]` / `--site LAT,LON [--radius M]` (2026-08-28) is the WAY-AUTHORITY scope**: the default scope SELECTS (apron-interior nodes the scaffold lifted and the build shipped near the terrain); this one selects NOTHING and asks the same questions of a way the owner NAMED, whatever its role and wherever it sits — per node the emitted value, the DEM under it, every enforced edge the sidecar recorded with its budget and whether it is TIGHT at exit, and the sidecar's own clamp / apron-seniority records. Same `load_arm`, same frame, no second instrument; it prices no law and counts no defects (counts come from `tools/harness/census.py`). Promoted from the hecar2 lane's `tmp/way_authority_read.py` on its SECOND use per RULINGS `7e90032`. Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_apron_pull_attrib.py`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/band_clamp_attrib.py` | Two questions about an emitted patch that no other indexed instrument answers, over the patch and its `.axes.json` sidecar only. (1) **Who is the band clamping, and does the band state a law for it?** For every `band_clamp_nodes` record: role, side, \|dz\|, and the distance from the clamped node to the AIRSIDE PAVEMENT DOMAIN the band of record is defined over — the role set IMPORTED from the band engine (`raster_reach_band.band_domain_roles`), never re-typed, because a second hand-written role list is the census-wrapper defect. This is the read that attributed the 2026-08-25 HECA roads round: 110 clamp records, 92 of them road-family, every floor-side road clamp inside the raster band's 30 m off-net radius and none outside it, against a band whose domain excludes the road roles entirely — the evidence for spec `road-band-seal-scope-spec.md` §1. (2) `--contact-rings`: **which road rings SHARE AN EDGE with airside pavement** (RULINGS 2026-08-25b), by CANONICAL IDENTITY — `to_osm` deduplicates emitted nodes by their 11-decimal spelling, so a shared consecutive node-id pair IS a shared edge and no proximity enters the test. Reports the partners, the worst step on the shared CONTACT edge and the worst step anywhere on the ring, and — separately, never folded in — the NEAR-MISS rings (within `--near-miss-m`, no shared edge), which the ruling excludes and which are for the owner to rule on. `--site LAT,LON` prints the ring profile at one named coordinate (the before/after evidence for an owner-reported site). **It measures no law and counts no defects** — the census is the law instrument; geometry and the metre frame come from `check_grade._parse_osm` / `_ll_to_m_factory` about the sidecar anchor. Read-only: nothing is fetched, composed or written. `--site LAT,LON --road-profile`: **the owner's in-sim complaint as numbers** (RULINGS 2026-08-25g, *roads are laterally flat*) — for every road ring near a named site, LONGITUDINAL BUMPS (the change in edge grade from one station to the next: a bump is a KINK, not a slope) and LATERAL TILT (every ring pair running >=45 deg across the ring axis) quoted against `SERVICE_ROAD_MAX_TRANSVERSE`, plus site totals and how many lateral pairs sit over the limit. The axis reader and the >=45 deg classifier are IMPORTED from `grade_law` — the same two the `road_cross_section` census family is built on — so the before/after site evidence and the acceptance number describe one population. `--radius-m` sets the site radius (default 200). Added 2026-08-25 (promoted from the roads lane's `clamp_attrib.py` scratchpad probe on its second use, per the tool-discipline ruling). Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_road_band_seal_scope.py::TestSpellingsCannotDrift::test_the_attribution_tool_reads_the_engines_domain`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/apron_drape_read.py` | The census is GREEN and the owner says the aprons look wrong. A census answers *is the surface lawful*; it cannot answer *did the surface stop being a surface* — a defect the law no longer prices emits NO ROW by construction, which is exactly what the 2026-08-21c interior-5 % round was (HECA censused 1,116 airside against a 1,487 bar while whole apron rings drained onto the DEM). Reads an emitted patch and reports three numbers over its APRON ring vertices, all metres: `height_above_dem` (emitted elevation minus the DEM under it — its MEDIAN is how high the plateau stands, and a draping apron drops toward 0), `ring_relief` (per apron ring, p95-p05 of that height — how much one apron tilts and rolls; a draping apron inherits the terrain's relief and this rises) and `amp50` (per vertex, the peak-to-peak of that height inside a 50 m window ALONG ITS OWN RING — the local *uneven hills and valleys* reading; reported with its p95 because a few bad rings are what a median hides). **It measures nothing itself and adjudicates nothing**: elevations come verbatim from `check_grade._parse_osm` (the harness library's own parser), the DEM through `flat_site_sweep`'s own loaders and the engine's own `dem.alt((lon-tile_lon, lat-tile_lat))` call, and the metre frame is `check_grade._ll_to_m_factory` about the sidecar's anchor — a private parser or a second sampler here would be the census-wrapper defect. Read-only: nothing is composed, densified, fetched or written. `--dem-source airport-inset` (default) is THE SURFACE PRODUCTION GRADES ON where an inset is cached; `base` reads the tile `.hgt`; a missing surface REFUSES rather than substituting the other, because two arms on two DEM sources are not comparable. **These are not defect counts** — quote them only ARM TO ARM on identical options (pass two or more patches and the table prints the delta), never as a verdict. Added 2026-08-24 for the back-edge rescope's drape-recovery acceptance, where no indexed instrument could state the number the ruling was written from. Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_apron_drape_read.py`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/road_terrain_conformance.py` | The census is GREEN and the owner says the ROADS are wrong — "capped at a visibly low grade, cutting through hills" (docs/POSTMORTEM-20260831.md). This is the TERRAIN-CONFORMANCE INSTRUMENT owner ruling RULINGS 2026-08-31a requires, and the reason it must exist is that NO CENSUS CAN SEE THE DEFECT: a road planed flat through a hill breaks no grade law at all — it is the most lawful surface there is — while the owner's road law says a road FOLLOWS TERRAIN up to `SERVICE_ROAD_MAX_GRADE` (8 %) and is pinned ONLY where it meets airside pavement. Per road CHAIN (a connected run of `service_road` / `service_junction` rings, joined by SHARED NODE IDs and walked along the component's longest path, stationed by ring-centroid arclength — the road's own PATH, never a plan chord, which is the frame `free_road_profile` solves in): `dem_relief_m` (how much hill the chain crosses), `emitted_relief_m`, **`follow_ratio`** (emitted/DEM relief — 1.0 rides the hill, 0.0 is a plane through it, and this is the headline), `cut_max_m` / `fill_max_m`, \|emitted−DEM\| median + p95, emitted vs DEM grade (max and median), and `dem_followable_pct` — the share of steps whose DEM grade is ALREADY within the road cap, which is what turns "flat" into "flat where the law allowed it to climb". **It measures no law and counts no defects**: geometry and altitudes come from `check_grade._parse_osm`, the role from `check_grade.law_role`, the road family from `check_grade._ROAD_FAMILY_ROLES` (which IS `grade_law.ROAD_ROLES`), the metre frame from `check_grade._ll_to_m_factory` about the sidecar's anchor, and the DEM through `apron_drape_read`'s own loaders and `_dem_at` (the engine's own `dem.alt`) — every set, parser and sampler IMPORTED, never re-spelled (the census-wrapper precedent). Every report also carries the COMPOSITION-FREE reading over ALL road-family vertices — |emitted−DEM| median + p95, the count of vertices cutting deeper than 5/10/20 m, and the worst — because chains are arm-dependent (emit decimation drops collinear vertices, so a flattened road fuses differently) while that population is not. **TWO POPULATIONS (2026-08-31, linear-transport Batch 1).** A `PATCH.osm` carries auto_patch's road pavement; **`--levelled-roads <tile>/o4_levelled_roads.json`** carries the CORE-owned roads, which emit as mesh `INTERP_ALT` ring altitudes and leave NO patch rows at all — invisible to the patch mode, to `check_grade` and to every census (RULINGS 2026-08-31b "LEVERAGE THE CORE"; spec `docs/specs/linear-transport-redesign-spec.md` §2 item 4). The sidecar is written by `O4_Vector_Map.include_roads`' longitudinal clamp pass: per OSM WAY, the centerline stations it clamped (<=20 m apart, so the instrument outresolves `emit_decimate`'s 60 m chords), each station's lat/lon, the terrain under it and the altitude the road took. The SAME statistics are computed from those two numbers and the SAME `--rank` / `--site` / `--profile` / `--json` readers print them — no second instrument. Its frame is DECLARED and is NOT the patch frame: one WAY per chain (not a shared-node component), the profile UNBINNED at the clamp's own stations, the DEM as the BUILD sampled it (no `--dem-source` applies), and the cap judged against is the SIDECAR's own `grade_cap`, not this file's constant. A levelled-roads arm compares to another levelled-roads arm and NEVER to a patch arm; the report says so whenever both are printed. It also prints the CLAMP read: ways, stations, how many were clamped beyond the 0.01 m materiality floor, and the max lift and max cut. Three modes: `--rank` DISCOVERS a patch's hill sites (chains over `--min-relief`, longest first, with way ids, end lat/lon and the deepest-cut coordinate), and `--site NAME=LAT,LON` reads ONE PLACE across arms, control first, joined by PLACE and never by way id (`arm_site_read`'s frame rule — ids are arm-dependent); `--profile` prints each arm's station/emitted/DEM table. A site with no chain inside `--site-radius` is reported with its nearest distance, never as zero. `--dem-source airport-inset` (default) is THE SURFACE PRODUCTION GRADES ON; a missing surface REFUSES rather than substituting the other, because two arms on two DEM sources are not comparable. Numbers are comparable ARM TO ARM on identical options and nowhere else. **`--by-ref` (lane `v2roadcap2`, 2026-09-13, RULINGS 2026-09-13ab)**: the same population and the same samplers, grouped by SOURCE REF (`dsf:pol51`, `pav17`) — vertices, worst FILL and worst CUT off the DEM each with its own coordinate, |emitted-DEM| median and p95, worst first. The ref is the unit the owner names a site by and the unit a CLASSIFICATION fix moves; the chain is arm-dependent (emit decimation re-fuses it) and the whole-airport reading hides one road behind 800 lawful vertices. FILL is reported apart from CUT because a road held UP in the air (owner item 5, KCLT `dsf:pol51` +13.28 m) is invisible to a cut-only reading. Scout `v2roadcapkclt` wrote a one-off for exactly this; promoted on its second use (RULINGS `7e90032`). Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_road_terrain_conformance.py` (the DEM is INJECTED, so the law is stated on a surface the twin owns — no corpus, no network; the `--by-ref` grouping, the fill/cut split, the worst-vertex coordinate and this index row). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/flat_site_sweep.py` | Asking whether an airport is a FLAT SITE — a site (OTHH's class) whose CIFP thresholds agree and whose DEM shows no relief its own source class can be trusted for, so every metre the solver chases there is DEM noise. Runs `auto_patch.flat_site`'s detector IN-PROCESS across a set of airports and prints one table: the four signals (CIFP threshold spread + consensus Z0; plane-fit slope and p95−p5 relief over `pavement ∪ boundary`, judged against the DEM SOURCE CLASS's own noise floor, after the SEA BAND and the DSM-STRUCTURE TAIL are excluded — both fractions reported per row; the DEM-vs-instrument offset; the pack-object consensus) plus the verdict, flagged against the spec's recorded expectations. The 200 m margin ring is measured and printed as `s2_ring_*` CONTEXT with no gate power (v3 amendment (a)), and an airport the owner has DECLARED flat (`flat_site_declared`) reads `flat_declared` with the detector's own would-be verdict recorded beside it. **It measures nothing itself** — every number comes from the detector's own code path, the same functions the pipeline calls at its DEM-in-hand point, so a sweep row and a build's `site_class` sidecar record cannot disagree; a private re-implementation of a signal here would be the census-wrapper defect. NO BUILDS, NO NETWORK, NO WRITES: the DEM is read straight off disk and never composed, densified or fetched (composing runs production DEM prep, which is a build step and can touch the shared data repo). `--dem-source base` (default) reads the tile's base `.hgt`; `--dem-source airport-inset` reads the cached airport-elevation inset — THE SURFACE PRODUCTION GRADES ON where one exists — with the source class taken from `O4_Airport_Elevation_Insets._inset_bake_provenance_entry`, the same reader the bake stamps the DEM with, so the sweep sees exactly what a build would and inherits its gaps rather than papering over them. **Run both arms**: an airport can read differently on the two surfaces, and only the inset arm answers what a production build will do (measured 2026-08-09: OTBD's relief falls 9.78 → 7.02 m between them; CYXY's inset is 1 m HRDEM and short-circuits to `lidar_credible`). An airport with no CIFP file, no base DEM, or no cached inset for the arm requested reports `no_data` — never skipped silently. `--elevation-dir DIR` / `--dem-path TILE=PATH` supply a raster for a tile the shared corpus does not have, from a LANE-LOCAL directory (spec §3 amendment, owner 2026-08-09: the owner-named flat test airports VHHH/VMMC/YSSY/KSFO/KOAK/KBOS sit on tiles N22E113, S34E151, N37W123, N42W072, which are not in the corpus and must NOT be fetched into it as a sweep side effect — if they later become fixtures their tiles join through the recorded `--refresh-data` path). Every run prints a DEM PROVENANCE block naming, per tile, the raster read and whether it came from the corpus, a lane-local directory or an explicit path — a corpus row and a lane-local row are not the same kind of evidence. Spec: `Ortho4XP/docs/specs/flat-site-detector-spec.md` §3. Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_flat_site_detector.py`. |

## Build drivers

| Tool | Reach for it when |
|---|---|
| `Ortho4XP/tools/harness/build_airport.py` | **Default.** See above. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/full_airport_build.py` | LEGACY, superseded by the harness build entry. Kept only for old scripts that still call it; it makes none of the harness's refusals and carries an unstated inset-coverage frame gap (its standalone DEM prep degrades to the base surface on a cold cache with only a log line). Do not use it in new work. **Unguarded.** |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/build_target_osm.py` | Producing a compare-target OSM to pair with `compare_target.py`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/production_airport_patch.py` | You specifically need the single-airport rebuild through the **tile** prelude (insets, overlay, densification, smoothing). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/run_tile_build.py` | A headless whole-tile build with the tile's own config. For a RELEASE-frame tile build use `harness/build_airport.py --tile` (it also applies the owner's X-Plane install paths, without which auto_patch is silently skipped). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/run_tile_mesh_only.py` | Consumer-side mesh work: steps 1–2 only, no imagery. Arms THE shared-repo write guard (`harness/shared_repo_guard.py` — the same single implementation `harness/build_airport.py` uses): an unauthorised write into the shared data repo refuses at the call, the run audits a full before/after snapshot, joins the bathymetry prefetch before disarming, and a refusal the engine swallowed fails the run. Measured precedent 2026-08-08: two unguarded mesh-only runs silently rewrote five inset/bathymetry manifests in the shared repo. It has NO refresh mechanism of its own — warm a cold tile with `harness/build_airport.py --refresh-data <scope>`. **`--patches-as-is` (2026-09-04, v2 M2 mesh A/B):** step 1 does NOT resolve the X-Plane install paths, so auto_patch generation is skipped and the `Patches/` files ALREADY ON DISK are meshed as they are — the deliberate measurement of a given patch's mesh-apply cost (Triangle input segments / constrained edges, step 1 and step 2 wall), printed as such; a run without the flag still refuses when no install resolves. Measured 2026-09-04 on +60-136, one run per arm: v1 CYXY patch (09-02 sweep arm) 108,708 input segments / 247,315 constrained edges, step 1 39.4 s, step 2 9.3 s; v2 M2 patch 105,420 / 243,088, step 1 34.8 s, step 2 8.8 s (both arms flagged by the guard for the same blocked `N60W136_airport_insets/index.json` warm-pass rewrite — identical frame both sides, not a patch effect). A third argument `first_step` (2026-08-11, round 15) selects the MESH REPLAY: `2` runs step 2 alone on the `.node` / `.poly` / `.weight` / `.alt` already in the build directory, which is the acceptance loop for a change in the mesh CONSUMER — re-running step 1 rewrites the very inputs under test, and in a checkout that resolves no X-Plane root it skips auto_patch entirely and still exits 0 (measured +25+051: 191,764 input vertices and 696,507 triangles against the 224,818 / 3,149,297 of the build being reproduced, and the patch-dependent degeneracy simply absent). Copy a build's four input files beside its `Ortho4XP_+XX+YYY.cfg` into a lane-local build directory and the replay meshes exactly the geometry that build meshed. **THE WINDOW IS NOT THE AUTHOR, here too (2026-09-14, lane meshscope):** the entry passes its INPUT SET to the audit — `shared_repo_guard.tile_input_scope`, the SAME `BuildInputScope` `build_airport.py` passes, derived for a whole tile: the tile + its seam neighbours in both spellings, the road feeds of the airports the engine's own cached per-tile dictionary names (`flat_site_mode.tile_icao_candidates`; none cached ⇒ every road feed stays in scope), and the mod-cache PACKS that have cached anything for those tiles (`mod_cache_packs_naming`, one directory listing at arm time — a pack's hash-keyed `o4_object_partition_/pad_frame_/exclusions_<hash>.cache` sidecars name no tile, so the PACK is the scope they carry). Measured 2026-09-13: a +40-004 LEMD run with its own `guard.blocked` EMPTY failed rc 1 CONTAMINATED on 117 HECA-pack sidecars + 1 OTHH path a concurrent lane's object stage wrote; those are now NAMED as EXTERNAL CANDIDATES (rc 0), an in-scope write still refuses, and a guard-blocked run externalises nothing (`blocked=guard.blocked` rides along). Twins: `tests/test_harness.py` (the pack rule, in-scope contamination, `packs=None` old strictness, `icaos=None`, the factory on a tmp repo, the entry's source). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/fetch_airport_elevation_insets.py` | Inspecting an airport's inset cache. It WRITES into the shared data repo, and outside the lock and the ledger — so for warming a cache use `build_airport.py --refresh-data dem --warm-insets ICAO`, which runs the same fetch for exactly that airport under the scope lock, hash-stamped into the shared refresh ledger. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/inset_coverage_census.py` | Choosing or auditing an airport-elevation-inset STALENESS rule, or asking what the cached insets actually cover. READ-ONLY: it walks every cached inset raster on the corpus, never queries an uncached OSM layer, runs inside a refusing `harness/shared_repo_guard.SharedRepoWriteGuard` and prints `[guard] blocked writes: N` last (non-zero = a defect in the tool). Three boxes per raster, never conflated — `boundary` (today's OSM aerodrome bounds, no margin), `requested` (what the fetch asked the provider for, the manifest's `bounding_box_wgs84`), `delivered` (the raster's own geotransform). `--section sweep` counts, per functional margin in `--margins`, the rasters failing to contain boundary+margin judged against the DELIVERED box with a one-delivered-pixel-per-axis tolerance (that tolerance is the whole trick: it needs no `native_resolution_m`, which the tnm_cog manifests do not carry); `--section battery` prints the delivered margin per edge for named airports; `--section delivery` asks whether a provider hands back less than it was asked for (the re-cut LOOP class — measured 2026-09-17: none does, worst 0.5 delivered pixels); `--section shipped` reproduces the rule that ships today, buckets its stale set by shortfall magnitude, and classifies WHICH INPUT MOVED (margin setting vs boundary moved). Measured 2026-09-17 over 565 rasters: 0 stale at every m_min ≤ 1000 m, 110 at 2000 m; 215 under the shipped rule (66 england1m cut under a 1000 m margin, 1 real boundary growth, 148 boundary movement ≤ 3.06 m). Twin `tests/test_inset_coverage_census.py` pins `expand_box` against the engine's own `_airport_bounding_boxes`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/fast_suite.sh` | The development fast lane. The full suite stays the merge gate. |

## Measurement discipline

| Tool | Reach for it when |
|---|---|
| `Ortho4XP/tools/run_with_ledger.py` | Any expensive CORRECTNESS run (pytest, airport build, check_grade). Skips a run another session already passed at an identical tree + `O4_*` env. The tree hash covers EXACTLY `CODE_PATHS` — its temporary index is seeded EMPTY (`git read-tree --empty`) before `git add -A -- <CODE_PATHS>`; seeding it from `HEAD` left the whole tree's `docs/` blobs in the index and any DOCS-ONLY commit moved the "code" hash (measured 2026-08-30: `docs/RULINGS.md` 5b552ae1 moved the key 2f56b778… → b1ec5ef8… and the artifact ledger refused a valid HECA control with CONTAMINATED-KEY). `--history` before repeating anything expensive. Never wrap a timing run. **PER-ROUND COST (2026-08-13, P2 instrument item 3):** `O4_ROUND_TAG` labels every executed run with the session/round that paid for it, and `--round TAG` (a readout, usable alone) lists that round's records and prints `round <tag>: N run(s), Xs wall`; every readout prints the per-round totals, untagged runs included, so a phase report sums cost per round without a second tool. The tag is a RECORDED FIELD, never part of the key: `relevant_env()` — the one place the key's env component is built, and the same one `harness/artifact_ledger.py` calls — filters `KEY_EXCLUDED_ENV = ("O4_RUN_LEDGER_PATH", "O4_ROUND_TAG")`. Keyed, it would invalidate the WHOLE ledger the moment a round started and re-run every already-passing verification, making the instrument that prices rounds the largest cost in one. A HIT appends no record (a hit costs the round nothing), so the totals sum real executions only. Twins: `tests/test_run_with_ledger.py` (`test_round_tag_is_recorded`, `test_round_tag_does_NOT_invalidate_the_ledger`, `test_history_attributes_wall_to_a_round`). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/profile_airport_build.py` | Attributing an airport build's wall time (sampling profiler, < 1 % overhead). `CallCounter` / `_install_counters` take an optional `clock` (2026-08-13, perf P3 lane G): default `time.perf_counter` (WALL, what the sampler and a build's own numbers are), `time.process_time` for a CPU-bound sink measured while other lanes hold the machine. The clock rides on the counter as `clock_name` so a report can never present one as the other; `tools/object_pad_evidence_report.py --count` is the first caller. **`--replay CAPTURE_DIR` profiles a SOLVE-STAGE REPLAY instead of a whole build (2026-08-13, perf P3 lane C):** same sampler and report, target `solve_cut.replay` — IMPORTED, never re-spelled, so the run still owes its `--baseline`/`--baseline-manifest`+`--baseline-key` body hash and still refuses env drift (`--allow-env-drift`). That is the optimisation loop's instrument: the solve sinks live in phases [5]+[6] and a whole build to see them costs ten times the wall. Naming both an ICAO and `--replay` (or neither) REFUSES — a profiler that silently picks one of two targets is how a lane reads a build's distribution and calls it a replay's. Phase attribution is one bucket on the replay path (a replay enters below pipeline.py's step boundaries) and the report says so. **Read its line attribution with care inside numpy-heavy loops:** the sampler thread can only run when the main thread yields the GIL, so it over-attributes to whichever statement holds the longest GIL-releasing C call — lane C measured 6.0 s on a box-clamp gather that direct `perf_counter` instrumentation priced at 0.31 s. Trust it for function-level ranking; confirm any single hot LINE with a timer before optimising it. **`--count-inputs MODULE:ATTR` is the DUPLICATE-WORK CENSUS (2026-08-13, perf P3 lane dupcensus)** — the instrument behind the owner's "are we computing anything twice per build, from lost caches, dead memos or repeated passes?", which assertion cannot answer. It is `--count` plus an INPUT FINGERPRINT taken BEFORE each call (after the call the inputs may have been mutated), and reports per callable: calls, DISTINCT fingerprints, DUPLICATE calls (a fingerprint already seen in this run) and the inclusive seconds those duplicate calls spent. The fingerprint is structural and BY VALUE — scalars/str/bytes by value, shapely geometries through `shapely.to_wkb` (ISO WKB, so type and dimension are in the digest), numpy arrays through dtype + shape + `tobytes`, tuples/lists/dicts recursively in order, sets by their SORTED member digests. An input with no value rule makes the WHOLE call **UNFINGERPRINTABLE**: still counted as a call, never joining a duplicate population, nothing guessed — a fabricated duplicate is a fabricated defect, and this instrument's entire product is a list of suspected ones. **`--count-inputs-identity`** is the same census with a `type:id(obj)` fallback for objects with no value rule; those calls' duplicates land in their OWN column and mean only "the same OBJECT was handed in again", a weaker claim than "the same VALUE", and the two columns are never added together. That mode is what makes a context-taking callable (`shape_constraints`, `final_grade_projection`, the unified-graph builders) measurable at all. **THE id() REUSE TRAP is closed and the closing is why identity numbers may be quoted:** CPython recycles a freed object's address, so a callable handed a FRESH short-lived object every call gets the same `id()` again and again — measured on this lane's FIRST HECA replay census, `grade_graph.shape_constraints` read 12,078 calls / 1,009 distinct / **11,069 identity duplicates worth 47 s**, a headline finding that was an allocator artifact; with one strong reference held per identity priced, the same replay reads 12,078 / 12,078 / **0**. Twin (`test_identity_mode_cannot_mint_a_duplicate_from_id_reuse`) carries the 200-object case plus a control proving the platform really does recycle. The cost is memory — one reference per DISTINCT object, live for the run — paid knowingly. **A `from module import name` caller is rebound onto the wrapper** (`rebind_aliases`, by OBJECT IDENTITY only, every moved binding recorded on the counter and PRINTED with the report): wrapping `MODULE:ATTR` replaces one module-dictionary entry, so a consumer that bound the original at ITS import time still calls the original — `finalize.py` binds `elevation._drop_overlap_against_fixed_shapes` and `elevation._load_airport_dem` exactly so, and without the rebind the census reported two of three calls and looked right doing it. The census is OBSERVATION-ONLY and that is twinned: the wrapper returns exactly what the wrapped callable returns, fingerprinting reads inputs through pure accessors, a fingerprinting FAILURE degrades to counting (never to skipping), and the seconds spent fingerprinting are measured separately and EXCLUDED from the inclusive totals, so a duplicate-seconds figure is the wrapped work's own time. **`--count-clock {wall,cpu}`** exposes the counters' clock on the CLI (`cpu` = `time.process_time`, the clock to quote when other lanes hold the machine). Both profilers share ONE implementation of the arming and the report block (`install_census_counters` / `census_report_lines`, imported by `profile_tile_build.py`); a spec named in two `--count*` lists is REFUSED, because double-wrapping counts every call twice and halves the duplicate fraction. Twin: `tests/test_profile_input_fingerprint.py`. Never run it through the run ledger: it measures wall time. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/profile_tile_build.py` | The whole-tile companion; samples every thread. **It REFUSES a tile with no CIFP (2026-08-13, P2 instrument item 4):** the dev tree and every lane worktree ship `cifp_data_path` EMPTY, `run_auto_patch_generation` then never calls the generator, and the P1 profiler measured a tile with NO auto_patch surfaces at all and exited 0. The refusal is `harness/build_airport.py`'s own `apply_xplane_install_paths` — IMPORTED, not copied (a slightly different copy of a harness law is the census-wrapper defect) — called before the `Tile` is built and before step 1, exactly as `build_tile` calls it; the resolved install paths are printed and written into the report header, so a profile always says which auto_patch frame it was taken in. Never run it through the run ledger: it measures wall time. **It ARMS THE SHARED-REPO WRITE GUARD since 2026-08-13 (perf P3 lane T) — it did not before, and that was a hole, not a doc slip:** it runs the same four steps as `harness/build_airport.py --tile`, auto_patch's ProcessPool included, which is the exact writer that put eight `Airport_mod_cache/*/o4_object_footprints_*` sidecars into the shared corpus from `run_tile_mesh_only.py` with `guard.blocked` EMPTY. Both halves are armed the same way that entry arms them (`redirect_engine_caches` at module scope before the engine import, then the guard, the before/after snapshot, the bathymetry-prefetch join, the swallowed-refusal detector) and the refusal is `shared_repo_guard.require_no_unauthorised_writes`, one spelling for both entries. **`--count MODULE:ATTR`** (repeatable, `ATTR` may be dotted to reach a method) gives call counts + INCLUSIVE `perf_counter` seconds, the CallCounter IMPORTED from `profile_airport_build.py`; **`--count-inputs` / `--count-inputs-identity` / `--count-clock`** are the DUPLICATE-WORK CENSUS on the tile side, the same arming and the same report block imported from that profiler (`install_census_counters` / `census_report_lines`) — see its row for what the columns mean; **`--no-sample`** drops the sampler, which on this tile's vector step roughly DOUBLES it (pure-Python hot loop) — a `--count` measurement run must not carry it, a distribution read should. Twins: `tests/test_profile_tile_build_refusal.py` (import-not-copy identity, reason string equal to the build entry's, applies-when-set, ordering before any step). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/patch_provenance.py` | Asking what a patch was built from: git sha, dirty flag, gate configuration, which insets baked into its DEM. Root-line only, so it is fast over a whole patch tree. Swept 2026-08-06: an absent stamp now names the condition actually verified (no such file / a directory / empty / no `<osm>` root / an `<osm>` root with no `o4_provenance_*` attributes) instead of the catch-all "unstamped or unreadable", and the raw-DEM line states the fact while `--strict-raw` alone carries the severity. The exit-code contract its docstring advertises for CI (0 clean / 1 dirty-or-unstamped / 2 could-not-read) is twinned in `tests/test_forensic_tools_instrument.py`. |
| `tools/blast.py` | **Before editing anything under `Ortho4XP/src/` or `Sources/`.** Direct importers, tests to run, role-literal / env-flag / wire-protocol hazards, co-change neighbours. `--audit` checks its own recall. **`--tests-for FILE [FILE...]` (2026-08-12, BS1) is the SWEEP SELECTOR:** it reads the diff (`--since REF`, default HEAD), works out which top-level symbols actually moved (AST before/after, so a reformat or a comment edit selects nothing) and prints the test files whose recorded symbol USE intersects them — measured on `auto_patch/layout.py` with a one-symbol change: **6 test files against the 113-file full direct-importer sweep**. With no FILE it selects for every `.py` the diff touched. The law is a UNION of four clauses and RECALL OVER PRECISION: symbol-attributed tests, ∪ ALL direct-importer tests of a changed file with at most `--cheap-ceiling` (15) of them, ∪ ALL of them for any symbol the index cannot attribute (dynamic use, a re-export, `__all__`, a module-level edit — the fallback is named on stderr, never a silent narrowing), ∪ changed test files. STDOUT is the pipeable list (`| xargs venv/bin/python -m pytest`), the stamped header and every fallback go to STDERR. `--audit --mutations N` is the twin that keeps it honest: it deletes one hot symbol per run AT RUNTIME (a pytest plugin — no source file is ever rewritten, because lanes build against this same tree), runs the sample's FULL sweep, discounts the unmutated baseline and requires every failing file to be IN the selection; recall 100 % is the acceptance and precision is printed beside it, plus what clause 1 alone would have caught. A mutation set with NO failing test is a FAIL, not a pass. Index shards are v3: v2 added `symbol_tests` / `symbols_attributed`; **v3 (2026-08-21) adds `tests_via_fixture` — FIXTURE-MEDIATED reach.** pytest wires tests to conftest by NAME, not import: `conftest.cached_airport_layout` imports `auto_patch.pipeline` inside its body, and a test that says `from conftest import cached_airport_layout` (or takes a fixture parameter, or `getfixturevalue`s it) never appears as an importer of anything pipeline transitively imports. On 2026-08-20 a lane editing `runway_segments.py` + `gap_fill.py` ran the listed sweep (472 passed) and skipped `test_pavement_grade.py` / `test_single_graph_acceptance.py` that way. The card now carries a separate `TESTS VIA CONFTEST FIXTURE` line (grouped by helper, not truncated), `--tests-for` adds a 5th union clause (`via-fixture=N`, the files named on stderr), and `--audit` carries two via-fixture canaries (`FIXTURE_CANARIES`). Src->src edges only (a tool importing a module is not a fixture path); helper->helper calls and fixture->fixture params are closed over. Rebuild stays ~2.1 s. An older index on disk rebuilds itself. |

## Audits and forensics — no hard gate

| Tool | Reach for it when |
|---|---|
| `Ortho4XP/tools/wedge_audit.py` | Hunting near-zero-angle wedges / epsilon divergence. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/flex_audit.py` | Diffing one patch against another: step 1 is the DISPLACEMENT MAP (values matched by rounded lat/lon, geometry being arm-independent), step 2 asks whether the taxi axes feeding each flexed cluster were at cap (the FLEX-LAST law). `--roles all` widens step 1 from the runway family to every emitted role and `--map-only` stops after it — the "did this change move the surface, and by how much" read (count over `--tol`, default 0.10 m; pass 0.01 m for a materiality read, plus worst and p50/p95). `--map-json OUT.json` additionally dumps the map ITSELF — every moved node's lat/lon and its SIGNED displacement, with the frame (both shas, the join rounding, the tol, the role scope) — because a question of the form "did this site move UP in one world and DOWN in the other" is a per-node join and cannot be read off p50/p95 (cycle 11 used it to measure the airside-view step displacing 97.4 % of shared apron sites in OPPOSITE directions between the two constant-DEM worlds). The dump is the printed summary's own input, never a re-derivation. **The join is lat/lon at 6 dp ≈ 0.11 m cells, so joining a metre-space site list to it through a lat/lon round-trip loses almost everything (measured: 2 of 360 endpoints recovered). Go through the patch's own node table instead — that control recovers 100 %.** Swept 2026-08-06: step 2 reads the LAW sidecar spelling `axes_exact` first (it read only the legacy `axes`, so a modern sidecar yielded ZERO axes and every cluster printed "no taxi axis nearby" — a silent wrong answer that read as exculpatory); it now STAMPS how many axes loaded under which spelling, REFUSES on zero, announces a SKIP when the sidecar is absent instead of crashing after step 1 has printed, and reports grade / cap / slack as numbers — the adjudicating `<< TAXI NOT AT CAP` flag is gone, the law it asserted lives in `runway_redistribute`. Both patches' provenance shas are in the header. `--by-role` (2026-09-05, lane v2relaxfull2) prints the same map as ONE ROW PER ROLE — n matched / max |d| / moved ≥ tol, worst first — the "what moved between two arms, and did the runway?" reading (the scratch `dz_by_role.py` promoted on its second use; twin in `tests/test_forensic_tools_instrument.py`). |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/clearance_spike_audit.py` | Turning "spikes beside the pavement at HECA" into coordinates. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/clearance_conformance_audit.py` | Asking whether a clearance cut protects anything or just rides the DEM. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/object_seating_report.py` | "That building floats" → a row with lat/lon. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/object_pad_anchor_report.py` | The question is WHERE A PACK'S RENDER DATUM IS, and whether the solve can reach it — the measurement surface every object-pad design turns on (S5, RULINGS "OBJECT PADS: RELATIVE COUPLING"). A terrain-relative DSF object renders at `mesh(placement.lat, placement.lon) + AGL`, plus the authored `base_y` (`object_anchor.py:2411-2432`), so ONE point governs the elevation it is drawn at. This groups a pack's placements by their exact `(lat, lon, AGL)` spelling — the canonical identity rule, never a proximity join — and per ANCHOR DATUM reports the resources, the `.anchor_bak` BAKED resources and (with `--sidecar --icao`) the pad REQUESTS hanging off it, then answers the two questions a design cannot guess: **is it SHARED** (measured 2026-08-13: HECA's 385 baked resources hang off 4 datums, 191 of them off ONE; OTHH's 1229 off 93) and **is it IN THE SOLVE** — a datum is classified against a REAL emitted patch as hosted/unhosted, and a host role is marked `[solve]` or `[soft]` by reading `solver_primitives.PAVEMENT_ROLES` itself, because a `graded_strip` or `groundside_pavement` host carries the mesh value the renderer reads but NO solve variable to couple against (that distinction is what ruled S5's coupling unmintable at HECA: 1874 of 1883 requests hosted, 0 on a solve role). `--mesh` (repeatable) reads the datum's ACTUAL rendered ground from a built `Data+XX+YYY.mesh` and, with two or more, its build-to-build drift; `--tile-lat/--tile-lon` adds the auto-patch DEM at the same point, whose difference from the mesh is the error any PRE-SOLVE design must absorb (HECA's dominant datum: DEM 108.441, mesh 101.180, −7.261 m, identical across three builds). It measures nothing itself — placements through `obj8_reader.read_dsf_object_placements`, the patch through `check_grade._parse_osm` (the harness library), the DEM through `elevation._load_airport_dem`/`_sample_dem`. **GUARDED** when a DEM is read (`build_airport.arm_shared_repo_protection`); without `--tile-lat/--tile-lon` nothing that could write is touched and the arming is skipped. **`--eval-patch` adds the PATCH-EVALUATED ground at each datum (2026-08-14, S5v3)** — `auto_patch.patch_ground.PatchGroundField`, IMPORTED not re-spelled, so the tool and the build read the emitted surface the same way: innermost non-pad covering shape, its ring's own vertex altitudes, Delaunay-triangulated, barycentric at the query, in a LOCAL-METRE frame (which is `O4_Mesh_Utils`' `(lon*cos(lat0), lat)` up to a uniform scale, and Delaunay is scale-invariant). With `--mesh` that pair IS the emission-time ruling's pre-registered premise test, and it printed the verdict: at HECA's 2112 hosted datums |patch-mesh| p50 0.0293 / p90 0.6021 / max 4.4933 m against the 0.75 m `DSF_OBJECT_FOOT_PAD_RESIDUAL_M` cap, and 0.0000 at every one of the 1874 request-carrying datums; the 152 over-cap datums carry ZERO requests and are large non-planar `groundside_pavement`/`apron` rings. **`--eval-sensitivity`** re-evaluates under the two arms the rule could have taken — outermost host instead of innermost (moved nothing: 0.0000 max) and the raw `(lon,lat)` frame instead of metres (p90 0.3117, max 2.4793 m) — which is how the frame was shown to be load-bearing and the host choice not. **`--also-patch`** (repeatable) adds another patch of the SAME tile, because the mesher triangulates them together and a datum can be hosted by a neighbouring airport's shape. `--from-json` re-renders a dump build-free. ALWAYS pass a patch whose body hash you have checked against the frame you are quoting: measured on a non-baseline patch copy the same pack read 0 of 842 requests hosted, on the frozen 1.0.245 patch 1874 of 1883 — the identity-mismatch law, exactly. Twins: `tests/test_object_pad_anchor_report.py`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/object_pad_evidence_report.py` | The question is whether a pack's OBJ8 structure rings have any EVIDENCE that a BUILDING stands there — the R18-2 building-evidence ruling's measurement surface (owner 2026-08-11b). Per structure: the vertical-structure reading (its members' own ABOVE-GRADE extents, and the fraction of its hull those building-tall members cover), the OSM half (`--icao` joins the airport's mapped building footprints; without it the OSM column reads `-` and NO absence is claimed), the library-path vouching, and the full refusal ledger of every gate upstream. **It measures nothing itself**: the structures come from `dsf_reader.read_dsf_object_building_evidence`, which runs production's own pooling, partition and `object_footprints.structure_ring` (an `evidence_out` out-parameter, so refused structures are measured too), and the coverage is `object_footprints.tall_member_coverage`, the same function the gate calls — a private re-derivation of "how tall is it over its own footprint" would be the census-wrapper defect. It also prices the two pending default-OFF defences on the pack in front of you: for each candidate `--span-sweep` value it separates the rings the EVIDENCE gate already refuses from the marginal ones, which are the gate's actual COST, and `O4_DSF_OBJECT_CONNECTOR_PREFILTER=1` re-runs it for the prefilter's. That is how round 18 ruled both OFF at HECA (span: 6 rings caught at 300 m, 3 already refused, and all 3 marginal ones real buildings including a 60,392 m² shell with a 113 m member; prefilter: 192 of 336 evidence-vouched rings dropped — the documented EGGW/EGLL failure reproduced, because in a material-split pack every texture page spans the field). It is also what caught the NAME-VOUCHING trap: HECA's Tai Models pack files its whole airport under `Airport/Hangar_Tower/`, so a path-anywhere match vouched 667 of 817 rings, phantom pads included. **GUARDED** — it resolves and parses pack objects and (with `--icao`) loads OSM in process, so it arms `build_airport.arm_shared_repo_protection` (redirects + refuse-mode guard) and refuses a swallowed block, exactly as `classify_report.py` does; `--from-json` re-renders a dump build-free and guard-free. Twins: `tests/test_dsf_object_buildings.py` (`TestVerticalStructureEvidence`, `TestEvidenceNameVouching`, `TestPendingDefencesStayOffByMeasurement`). **It is also THE OBJECT-PARTITION SINK'S REPLAY (2026-08-13, perf P3 lane G):** the reader it drives (`read_dsf_object_building_evidence`) is deliberately UNCACHED, so it runs production's whole pooling / weld / contact-graph / narrow phase on one pack in ~70 s instead of a ~570 s build — the partition is not reachable from `solve_cut --replay` (it runs before step 1, not in phases 5-6). `--count MODULE:ATTR` (repeatable) reports each named callable's call count and INCLUSIVE seconds using `profile_airport_build.CallCounter` — IMPORTED, never re-spelled — clocked on `time.process_time`, because five optimisation lanes share one machine and a WALL total there reports their load as this function's cost (measured 2026-08-13: load average 32 moved a `contact_graph` total by 65 % between two identical arms). Every run prints `rings_sha256` / `rows_sha256`, the emitted ring set and the per-structure evidence rows hashed: that pair IS the semantic gate an optimisation of the partition machinery must hold fixed, checkable per iteration instead of per build. |

| `Ortho4XP/tools/decode_dsf_terrain_table.py` | Verifying an emitted DSF's terrain table / per-patch attributes. **`--water-datum-audit` (2026-09-17, lane `shorewater`, owner sim read at OTHH: "water goes up on to vertical parts of the land") is the DSF-SIDE half of the water-datum read, the half `mesh_region_tris.py --water-audit` structurally cannot see.** The mesh audit asks whether a water-BIT triangle carries a raised vertex; this asks whether a triangle DRAWN BY THE WATER SHADER does — and the two populations are not the same set, which is the whole defect: in `default_xplane`/`airport_ortho` a mesh LAND triangle takes its terrain from X-Plane's own landclass at its centroid, that coastline is far coarser than the mesh's, and `terrain_Water` on a bank triangle draws the water shader several metres up the bank while every mesh law is satisfied. Over every water-terrain triangle of a built DSF (the predicate is the ENGINE's own `O4_Default_Terrain_Map.is_water_terrain`, which is also what the writer filters on, so law and instrument cannot disagree) it reports, per emitting BAND (the patch's coordinate depth — 5 planes is the default-landclass LAND band written only by `emit_physical_default_land`, 7 the mesh-water band), how many stand over `--water-flag` (0.05 m), their ground area, the max height and the worst distinct sites. `--mesh` adds the band-free verdict over the RAISED population — each centroid classified land / inland / sea against the mesh's own water triangles through `mesh_region_tris._read_mesh_attributed` + `O4_DSF_Utils.remap_water_tri_type` (containment, never a coordinate join: DSF coordinates are 16-bit per-pool quantised (~1.5 m) and `recut_water_tris` inserts nodes the mesh never had, so a key join is unsound both ways). `raised_on_mesh_land` is THE acceptance number — raised INLAND water is lawful (each body keeps its own level), so a tile-wide "all water at 0" bar would be wrong. `--near LAT LON RADIUS_M`, `--band-depth N`, `--top`, `--json`. Measured on the shipped 1.0.344 OTHH tile +25+051 (`default_xplane`): 4,806 water-terrain triangles on the LAND band, 2,118 raised (3.36 km², max 62.95 m), 2,117 of them confirmed land by the mesh; the mesh side was already perfect (96,055 SEA triangles, every one of 62,951 vertices at 0.000). Twin: `tests/test_dsf_texture_modes.py::test_water_terrain_never_painted_on_a_land_triangle` (a synthetic raised bank over a landclass that calls it water, built through the real `build_dsf` twice — base commit vs edited — so the audit is proven to SEE the defect before it is used to declare it gone) and `::test_is_water_terrain_predicate`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/undulation.py` (`cd Ortho4XP && venv/bin/python tools/undulation.py PATCH.osm [PATCH.osm ...] [--role ROLE] [--top N] [--json OUT]`) | You need THE UNDULATION READ — the owner's 2026-09-08t complaint ("still follows the DEM too closely — unrealistic undulation") as a number, per ROLE and per way: the RMS SECOND DIFFERENCE of z along every chain (the grade change at each interior station, dimensionless) and the MAX / p95 GRADE CHANGE PER 100 m. Patch-only and law-free on purpose, so a v1 patch, a v2 patch and a v2 patch from another engine version are compared on exactly the same footing (both carry `alt_abs` per node and `role` per way). Born and promoted in lane `v2smooth` for the design-surface acceptance (spec `auto-patch-v2/design-surface-spec.md` §5). Twin owed. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/pad_level_report.py` | You are asking WHERE A BUILDING PAD SITS and whether it is still ONE PLANE (owner RULINGS 2026-09-10l/10y). `pads` reads every pad's least-squares-plane residual, its tilt against the 1 % ceiling, what it FRONTS and how far the pavement's own level beside it stands from the contacts it shares; `transect` walks z over the patch's own triangulation from a pavement ring to a named vertex (the "does the apron fall into the terminal" read); `delta` joins two emitted patches per way and reports which pads moved, by how much, and how each ring's spread changed. Solve-arm pickles for the first two, emitted patches for the third. Prices no law, counts no defects. Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_pad_level_report.py`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/runway_end_ground.py` (`cd Ortho4XP && venv/bin/python tools/runway_end_ground.py PATCH.osm --end NAME LAT LON [--radius-m 500] [--level-m 0] [--roles ...] [--json OUT]`) | You are asking the rounds 17 / 17b / 17c question — how much of the GRADED GROUND within N metres of a named runway end sits AT OR BELOW a level it has no business being at (VHHH: 1,681 vertices at or below 0 m, the runway-end canyons). It measures EMITTED ALTITUDES near named points; it is not a grade law and not a census family. The patch is read through `check_grade._parse_osm`, the one reader, and there is NO baked end table — an end list inside an instrument is a second source of truth about where a runway is. Below-grade roles (`tunnel_trench` and family) are deliberately out of `DEFAULT_ROLES`: their below-grade population is lawful and would drown the signal. **`--corners ICAO`** (lane `v2rwycorner`, spec §35 / RULINGS 2026-09-13q item 1, promoted out of that lane's scratchpad on its second use): the RUNWAY-END CORNER read instead — per runway end AND SIDE (twelve rows for a three-runway airport), the graded-strip vertices beyond the end, lateral of the runway's own half-width, inside the zone-2 half-width and inside `end_skirt.corridor_length_m`, each priced at its step to the NEAREST POINT of the end edge over the true plan distance `d` against `end_skirt.max_down_grade · d + q`. No `--end` needed: the axis is fitted from the patch's own runway rings by the ENGINE's `principal_axis` arithmetic (twin-asserted equal), and the ruleset is the airport's, so an FAA airport is judged under FAA law. This is the instrument the §35 before/after quotes: KCLT 1.0.324 reads 7 corner vertices over their bound, worst 5.07 m over 3.91 m (bound 0.34) at 18C/36C's south end. Twin: `Ortho4XP/tests/test_runway_end_corners.py`. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/rwy_profile.py` (`cd Ortho4XP && venv/bin/python tools/rwy_profile.py PATCH.osm [--icao HECA] [--rwy 05C/23C] [--cross 05L/23R]`) | You need a RUNWAY PROFILE read off an emitted v2 patch — the crown spine's built z by station against the apt.dat threshold line: the ridge MINIMUM and its station, the BOW (min z − line), the z at a crossing runway's intersection (the owner's 109 m point at HECA), the max |grade| and the max grade change per 100 m (the K reading). These are the bow / ridge / K figures the HECA rounds quote (RULINGS 2026-09-06l..06x); reads the production DEM frame through `auto_patch_v2.airport.load`. Born in lane v2routecap's scratch, promoted on its second use (lane v2lexi). Twin owed. **`--binned [--compare OTHER.osm]`** (lane v2chord, RULINGS 2026-09-08d): the CROSS-ENGINE read — a v1 patch has no crown spine, so the ridge is the max z per 50 m station bin over the runway-role nodes within 40 m of the axis, for every two-threshold runway on this patch and the other: bow, ridge minimum, max |grade|, max grade change, z − DEM (mean/min/max) and the station table of both ridges and the DEM (scout `hecav1v2` `rwy.py` promoted on its second use; the spec heca-v1-parity §5 runway-profile acceptance reads through it). **`--target`** (lane v2rwycurve, spec §21 / RULINGS 2026-09-10t (3)): with `--binned`, also reads the built ridge against the TARGET PROFILE the design surface was given — the ground's long-wave trend through the threshold pins, fitted by `constraints/runway_chord`'s OWN `_Trend`/`_Chord` (never a second implementation of the fit) over this tool's binned DEM read: built−target RMS and max, how far the target sits off the straight chord, and a TARGET row in the station table. The straight-chord bow is kept beside it for continuity with every earlier round. |
| `Ortho4XP/tools/pad_airside_arm.py` | **THE PAD/AIRSIDE INVARIANCE ARM** (RULINGS 2026-09-14as (i), lane `v2padvert`; promoted from that lane's scratchpad on its eighth use per RULINGS `7e90032`). `V2PADVERT_ENGINE=<engine tree> venv/bin/python tools/pad_airside_arm.py ICAO OUT.json` — ONE load, then classify+planar TWICE (`[placement] pad_from_cluster` OFF then ON) and a diff of §20b stage 1's OWN airside vertex population (`solve/design_roles.airside_stage_vertices`, never a hand list) and the apron face list between the arms. It also prints `classify/evidence.PAD_AIRSIDE` (what the airside clip and the rim snap did, with every refusal by reason) and, per NEW airside vertex, its distance to the other arm's nearest airside VERTEX and to its airside BOUNDARY — the two readings that separate a clip crossing point from an identity-grid artefact. The report now also carries the arrangement's own `renode_deleted` / `renode_minted` counters (§16g (10) (12) (2)) beside the gone/new diff. MEASURED at HECA with the clip alone: 1,008 gone / 283 new before §16g (10) (12), **0 deleted / 40 minted** after it. |

`--why-from PKL --why-hump RUNWAY S0 S1 | --why-vertex V [--why-relax FAMILY ...]`; `--why-hard [N] [--why-hard-stage 1]` on either a `--replay` arm or a `--why-from PKL`; `--why-from PKL --probe-site LAT,LON [--probe-drop M] [--probe-arm TERM=V ...]`) | You are iterating on the v2 SOLVE (a law table, a generator, the shape stage, the planar build) and want the runway bows / z − DEM / joints / yielded-rows figures per change WITHOUT paying for the load stage each time — the synthetic-first solve arm (CLAUDE.md BUILD ECONOMY; `docs/specs/auto-patch-v2/heca-sag-ablation/ablate_heca_pin.py` was the scratch pattern, promoted on its second use by lane v2chord, RULINGS 2026-09-08d). `--capture` runs load → **the PACK PARTITION + GROUPS** (`build.py:288-318`, owner RULINGS 2026-09-11j — folded in 2026-09-12u by lane `v2padceiling` after scout `v2unsettled` measured the trap: a capture without them carries an `Airport` with no `partition` and no `groups`, so the replay silently solves a DIFFERENT problem — no `foot_rows`, no `pad_relief` targets, no basin bodies, and the shipped LEMD hard set could not be reproduced at all; a capture predating it is now REFUSED BY NAME at replay, `capture_has_groups`) → classify → planar (which labels the SHAPES, owner RULINGS 2026-09-08k) → flat site → road profile → shape stage exactly as `pipeline/build.py` and pickles the airport, the classification, the planar map and the stage (**and THE CLUSTERS beside the partition and the groups** — lane ``v2padjoin`` 2026-09-14: a capture without ``Airport.clusters`` leaves §30 (4)'s cluster pad, its apron reach and §16g (10)'s derived pads INERT in every replay of it, so a pads-ON arm silently measures the pads-OFF law; a capture predating them has them DERIVED at replay off its own partition, named on stdout) (HECA 56 s; LEMD 232 s, of which 104 s partition); `--replay` resumes from the named stage under the CURRENT tree (constraints + joint filter + yield transform + ONE solve by default — 08k (4), no joint re-solve; `shapes` rebuilds the shapes over the captured map and re-runs the stage — for a change in `planar/shapes.py` or `[terrace]`; `planar` the whole map), and prints per two-threshold runway the crown-ridge BOW, the ridge minimum, z − DEM mean/min/max, the law-tier line, `yielded_rows` per family and the max apron grade PER SHAPE with the steepest rows per family (face, grade, span, lat/lon), the shapes (count, the largest by vertices), the joints (count, gap joints, max built step, the worst with their shape pair), the road steps, and per `--site` the vertices within `--site-radius` (z − DEM, roles, shape ids, the max |dz| over a short edge = a step). `--emit DIR` writes the arm's patch through the build's own emit half so `patch_proximity_diff.py` and `undulation.py` can read a same-frame divergence on a replay arm; `--verify` runs the v2 VERIFY CENSUS on the solved surface and prints the rows per family and the DEFECT families the app's driver gates on (lane v2smooth round 2, RULINGS 2026-09-08v — a weight sweep needs the defect count per arm without a 130 s build); `--design-weight TERM=W` overrides one `emit.toml [design]` weight for the arm (the measurement arm, never a default). `--drop-generator` also accepts the pseudo-generator **`eat_ramp_reach`** (lane `v2eatramp`, spec §36 (5)): the EAT ramp's trend WITHDRAWAL is a channel edit made before the solve, not a row, so it cannot be dropped by the row filter — `eat_anchor_rect` drops the pins and their reach together, `eat_ramp_reach` the withdrawal alone (the §36 (5) before-arm). Lane v2shapes (2026-09-08): HECA capture 56 s; one pass 144–150 s (the 08g three-pass law read 357 s). `--why-from PKL --why-hump RUNWAY S0 S1` (the `solve.why` bindings and chain trace on the highest ridge vertex above the chord, from a duals solve of the SAME LP — kept out of the timed arm) with `--why-relax FAMILY ...` (relax-one-family arms: the hump's dz after a full re-solve without the family — the INTERVENTIONAL holder read). `--why-hard [N]` lists EVERY VIOLATED HARD ROW of the solved surface — generator, ruling, demanded vs allowed metres, and per vertex the id, coefficient, z, DEM, lat/lon and roles — re-assembling the design problem with `solve.design.assemble` and applying design.py's own `2 / Σ|c|` row scaling, so every violation reads in the METRES `hard_tol_m` is stated in (promoted from scout `v2unsettled2`'s `hardrows.py` on its second use, spec §32 (4), RULINGS 2026-09-13ac: `HARD SET NOT SETTLED` names ONE truncated ruling, which cost 12u a scout re-deriving the population by hand and 13ac needed the row's VERTICES to see that main's worst row was a pair the zone projection had clamped independently).  **`--why-hard-stage 1`** reads §20b STAGE 1's OWN ASSEMBLY instead of the full problem's (`solve.design.stage_split`'s drop + fixed): the AIRSIDE hard set, in the rows and the metre scaling stage 1 enforced them under. The full read cannot answer "does the AIRSIDE solve settle" — the airside rows are a subset of 325k whose population and scaling the stage's own drop changes — and that is exactly the claim RULINGS 13y (B) / 13ab / 14as make (lane `v2settle`, which used it to split HECA's "42 unsettled rows" into 9 CONSTANT rows the solve can never move, 6 HELD runway rows at the projection's own bar, and 18 real ones).
